Performing the simultaneous background-only fit for the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
selections,
normalisation factors for
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
of

1.07\pm 0.02
(

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

),

0.94\pm 0.03
(

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}-\scalebox{0.9}{{H}${}_{\text{T}}$}}}

) and

0.85\pm 0.05
(

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}-\scalebox{0.9}{{H}${}_{\text{T}}$}}}

)
are found.

A good description of the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
distributions in the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
simulation is crucial in this analysis,
especially in the high-
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and high-
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
tails where new physics may appear.
The tail of the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
distribution is a result of, in decreasing order of importance: the use of a wrong pair of leptons
to compute the mass of the
Z
-boson candidate and the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
of the
W
-boson candidate (‘mis-pairing’ of the leptons),
the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
resolution, and the
W
-boson width.
The prediction of lepton mis-pairing in simulation is validated in a control sample in data similar to the one used to calculate the cross-section normalisation factor,
but only allowing events with a SFOS pair of different flavour than the
W
lepton.
The
Z
-boson candidate can then be identified unambiguously,
and a mis-paired control sample is obtained using the DFOS pair in the
m_{\ell\ell}
computation and using the third lepton to calculate
m_{\mathrm{T}}
.
Finally, the modelling of the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
distributions is validated in a
W{+}\gamma
control sample.
The
W{+}\gamma
and
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
processes have very similar
m_{\mathrm{T}}
shapes because their production mechanisms are similar,
with the exception that the FSR production diagram of
W{+}\gamma
is much more common than the corresponding diagram in
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
,
which is doubly suppressed due to the mass of the
Z
boson and its weak coupling to leptons.
Furthermore, a photon is a good proxy for a leptonically decaying
Z
boson since photons and leptons are reconstructed with comparable resolutions,
and no large extra mismeasurements are expected.
The enhancement of the FSR diagram in the
W{+}\gamma
process leads to differences in the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
distribution shapes between
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and
W{+}\gamma
.
When a photon is radiated, leptons lose energy, resulting in a lower
m_{\mathrm{T}}
.
In order to use the
W{+}\gamma

m_{\mathrm{T}}
shape to validate the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
MC prediction, the FSR contribution in the
W{+}\gamma
control region has to be suppressed.
This is done by placing threshold requirements on the
p_{\text{T}}
of the photon,
p_{\text{T}}^{\gamma}>50
,
and the separation between the lepton and the photon,
\Delta R(\ell,\gamma)>0.4
, in
W{+}\gamma
events,
as FSR photons are expected to be close to the lepton radiating them and also tend to have low
p_{\text{T}}
.
The distribution shapes of
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, as well as other kinematic variables, are compared in data and MC events in the
W{+}\gamma
region.
The
m_{\mathrm{T}}
distribution in the validation region with mis-paired leptons and the
W{+}\gamma
validation region are shown in Figure
3
.
Good agreement in both control samples is observed and no extra corrections or scale factors are applied to correct the
m_{\mathrm{T}}
distribution for the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
background.

The
t\bar{t}
MC modelling is validated in VRs, enhancing the
t\bar{t}
contribution by requiring a DFOS lepton pair and using a moderate
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}>50\penalty\
selection.
The main VR,

VR

t
\bar{\texttt{t}}

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, requires the presence of one or two
b
-jets, further increasing the
t\bar{t}
contribution.
To validate the modelling in the
n_{\mathrm{jets}}=0
region as well,
an additional VR inclusive in
b
-jets,

VR

t
\bar{\texttt{t}}

The systematic uncertainties considered in the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
SRs follow the approach discussed in Section
6.3
.
The relative composition of FNP muons is similar between the

CR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

,
whereas for FNP electrons the main source in the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

is photon conversions,
while in the

CR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

the heavy-flavour decay contribution dominates.
An additional source of uncertainty that is considered accounts for the different FNP lepton compositions in the

CR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

.
This uncertainty arises from the method’s performance in the simulation (closure) in various regions of parameter space
and is given by the differences between the estimated and simulated yields of events in the given region.
In the DFOS region where the triboson contribution becomes dominant,
the uncertainties related to the QCD renormalisation and factorisation scales are also evaluated
for this background component, in the same way as previously described for diboson and
t\bar{t}
.
A summary of the considered systematic uncertainties is presented in Figure
5
,
with uncertainties grouped as discussed in Section
6.3
.

Bin-to-bin fluctuations in the statistical uncertainty as well as the experimental uncertainty reflect the difference in expected yields in the various search regions, which varies by an order of magnitude.
These uncertainties become the dominant ones in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

-3--4, 6--8, 11--12

and
15--16
of the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection,
and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{SFOS}}}

-5

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{SFOS}}}

-14

, and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{SFOS}}}

-19

of the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
selection, due to limited number of MC events at high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and
m_{\mathrm{T}}
.
Although the FNP lepton uncertainty is negligible in the majority of the search bins, its relative size reaches 30% in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{DFOS}}}

-2

,
due to the small number of events in the corresponding anti-ID sample.

Figure 5:

Breakdown of the total systematic uncertainties in the background prediction for the SRs of (left) the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection and (right) the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
selection.

8
Off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection

The following subsections discuss the implementation specific to the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection,
expanding on the general strategy outlined in Section
6
.
The selection is applied on top of the common preselection as defined in Section
5
,
and the SRs are optimised to the wino/bino (+) scenario.

8.1
Search regions

The

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

selection targets the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
region
by requiring
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}},m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{max}}<75\penalty\
.
The
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{max}}
is the largest SFOS lepton pair invariant mass in the event,
and the double requirement helps to maximally suppress combinatorial backgrounds with an on-shell
Z
boson.
Further variables used in the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection assume
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
-based lepton assignment to the
Z^{*}
- and
W^{*}
-boson candidates unless otherwise indicated.
The event preselection vetoes events with a
b\text{-jet}
to reduce contamination from
t\bar{t}
,

requires the three leptons to be well separated in
\text{min}\penalty\ \DeltaR_{\mathrm{3\ell}}=\min[\Delta R(\ell_{i},\ell_{j});\text{for all lepton pairs}\penalty\ \scalebox{0.9}{$(\ell_{i},\ell_{j})$}]
,

and requires a lower bound on
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
of 1 to remove events with collimated leptons for which FNP lepton background estimation is challenging.
Finally,
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
mass ranges of
[3.0,3.2]
and
[9,12]
are vetoed to avoid contributions
from
J/\psi
and
\Upsilon
resonance backgrounds associated with a FNP lepton,
except in the jet-inclusive high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
regions (
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}>200\penalty\
) where the contribution is negligible.

Preselected events are further divided into four categories based on the multiplicity of jets with
p_{\text{T}}>30\penalty\
(
n_{\text{jets}}^{30\penalty\ }
), and on
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
Jet-veto categories

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

reject events containing jets and select low and high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, respectively.
Jet-inclusive categories

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

require at least one jet and also separate the events with low and high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
As the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
is harder in the jet-inclusive categories, due to the recoil between the
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
system and the jets,
the boundary between the low and high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
bins is set at 50 for the jet-veto categories and at 200 for the jet-inclusive categories.
The

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

primarily target signals with moderate mass splitting (
\Delta m\sim[40,90]\penalty\
),
and rely mostly on moderate kinematics and lepton triggers.
The

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

also target signals with highly compressed mass spectra (
\Delta m\lesssim 40\penalty\
) – resulting in events with very soft leptons –
by exploiting events with large
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
recoiling against hard hadronic activity.
Initial lepton
p_{\text{T}}
requirements are kept as loose as possible,
p_{\text{T}}>10\penalty\
for

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

, and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

, and
p_{\text{T}}>4.5\,(3.0)\penalty\
for
e
(
\mu
) in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

;
however, the selection is restricted by the trigger requirements (Section
5
) and some further requirements are applied in the bin-by-bin SR optimisation as discussed in the following.

Further preselection criteria are applied to reduce the contamination from
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
.
First, a lower bound is set to ensure
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}\penalty\ \mbox{significance}>1.5
or
3.0
, depending on the SR category.
For

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

, events are then treated separately for different flavours of the lepton from the
W
-boson decay (
\smash{\ell_{\mathrm{W}}}
),
selected using
\smash{m_{\ell\ell}^{m_{Z}}}
-based lepton assignment to best capture the SM background topology for rejection.
To suppress the contribution from
Z(+\gamma)\rightarrow\ell\ell ee
caused by bremsstrahlung from prompt electrons and subsequent photon conversions,
if
\smash{\ell_{\mathrm{W}}}
is an electron,
the trilepton invariant mass
m_{3\ell}
is required to be off the
Z
-boson peak (
|m_{3\ell}-m_{Z}|>20\penalty\
),
and the minimum angular distance between all SFOS lepton pairs must be within
\text{min}\,\DeltaR_{\mathrm{SFOS}}\in[0.6,2.4]
,
with
\text{min}\,\DeltaR_{\mathrm{SFOS}}
defined as
\min[\Delta R(\ell_{i},\ell_{j});\text{for all SFOS lepton pairs}\penalty\ \scalebox{0.9}{$(\ell_{i},\ell_{j})$}]
.
The preselection criteria and categorisation are summarised in Table
8
.

The primary discriminant in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

is
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
.
This variable serves as a proxy for the mass splitting of the targeted signals,
and displays a characteristic kinematic edge at their mass-splitting value:
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}=\Delta m
,as demonstrated in Figure
6
.
A shape fit over the
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
spectrum is performed in each SR category.Seven
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins are defined with boundaries at 1, 12, 15, 20, 30, 40, 60 and 75 , and labelled

‘a’

to

‘g’

;
the
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bin labels are added to the region names as defined above.
Signal regions

‘a’

are dropped everywhere except in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

,
to avoid low-mass resonance backgrounds.

A second, similar kinematic edge is present in stransverse mass
m_{\mathrm{T2}}

[
174
,
175
]
,
reflecting the kinematic constraint originating from the
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\rightarrow W\smash{{}^{*}}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
decay chain.
In this selection,
m_{\mathrm{T2}}
is constructed by assigning the dilepton system providing
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
(
\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
) to one visible particle leg,
and the remaining lepton (
\ell_{3}
) to the other leg:

m_{\mathrm{T2}}^{m_{\chi}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}},\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell_{3}},\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{miss}}\right)=\underset{\mathbf{q}_{\mathrm{T}}}{\min}\left(\max\left[m_{\mathrm{T}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}},\mathbf{q}_{\mathrm{T}},m_{\chi}\right),m_{\mathrm{T}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell_{3}},\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{miss}}-\mathbf{q}_{\mathrm{T}},m_{\chi}\right)\right]\right),

where the transverse mass
m_{\text{T}}
in this
m_{\mathrm{T2}}
formula is defined by

m_{\mathrm{T}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell},\mathbf{q}_{\mathrm{T}},m_{\chi}\right)=\sqrt{m_{\ell}^{2}+m_{\chi}^{2}+2\left(\sqrt{(p_{\text{T}}^{\ell})^{2}+m_{\ell}^{2}}\sqrt{q_{\mathrm{T}}^{2}+m_{\chi}^{2}}-\mathbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\ell}\cdot\mathbf{q}_{\mathrm{T}}\right)}.

A hypothesised mass
m_{\chi}
is assigned to each invisible particle leg, corresponding to the
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
mass;

m_{\chi}
is fixed to 100 in this selection.
7
7

7

The dependency of the performance on hypothetical invisible particle mass
m_{\chi}
is generally small
except when assuming
m_{\chi}\sim 0\penalty\
for signals with finite
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
mass, where the signal kinematic edges become significantly smeared.

The kinematic edge for signals appears at
m_{\text{T2}}^{100}=\Delta m(\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2},\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1})+100\penalty\
as illustrated in Figure
6
.
To take advantage of this feature, a sliding cut is applied per
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bin, requiring
m_{\text{T2}}^{100}
to be smaller than the upper
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bin edge + 100 .
SM backgrounds can exceed the boundary and are suppressed, while a large fraction of the signal contribution targeted by a given bin is retained.
The cut is particularly effective in the lowest
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins, targeting the smallest mass splittings:
e.g. in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

a

(
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}\in[1,12]\penalty\
) the total background is reduced by a factor of three following
m_{\text{T2}}^{100}<112\penalty\
,
while the efficiency for
\Delta m=10\penalty\
signals is
>95\%
.

Figure 6:

Distributions of (left)
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
and (right)
m_{\text{T2}}^{100}
showing the expected SM background as well as signals with various mass splittings
\Delta m(\smash{\raisebox{-0.65pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}$,\,$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}$ \hskip-1.79993pt}})
(
m(\hskip 1.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}$}}$ }\hskip-2.0pt)=m(\hskip 1.0pt\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}$ }\hskip-2.0pt)=200\penalty\
),
for a selection of exactly three baseline and signal leptons.
The distributions are normalised to unity.
Signals demonstrate a cut-off in both variables matching the mass splitting, while backgrounds do not.
The dominant background in this selection is
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, with the
Z
-boson mass peak visible in both distributions.

Event selection is tightened further by employing various background rejection criteria,
optimised separately for each

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

category and each
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bin.
The discriminating variables used and the detailed bin-by-bin cut values are summarised in Table

.

In order to reduce the FNP lepton background contributions from
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
and
t\bar{t}
,
lepton
p_{\text{T}}
thresholds are raised in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

.
In these same three categories, the transverse mass
m_{\text{T}}^{\text{mllmin}}
is used to suppress the SM
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
contribution;
the
m_{\text{T}}^{\text{mllmin}}
variable is constructed using the
W
lepton after
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
-based lepton assignment
and marked with ‘

mllmin

’ to distinguish it from the
m_{\text{T}}
variable in the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection.
The SRs target phase space either below or above the SM
W
-boson peak present at
m_{\text{T}}^{\text{mllmin}}\sim m_{W}
.
An upper bound of
m_{\text{T}}^{\text{mllmin}}<
50–70 is applied in low
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins,
while the

‘f’

and

‘g’

bins are split into two parts below (

‘f1’

,

‘g1’

) and above (

‘f2’

,

‘g2’

) the Jacobian peak of SM
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
.

In

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

, the selection on
\text{min}\,\DeltaR_{\mathrm{SFOS}}
is tightened in the low
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins, exploiting the topology with a relatively boosted
Z^{*}
in the target signatures,
and a lower bound on
m_{3\ell}
is applied for the high
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins to reject the SM
Z\rightarrow 4\ell
background peaking at
m_{3\ell}\sim m_{Z}
.
The ratio of the magnitude of a vectorial
p_{\text{T}}
sum of the three leptons,
|\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{lep}}|
, to
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, is labelled
|\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{lep}}|/E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and
represents the extent to which the transverse momentum of the hard-scatter
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
system, recoiling against ISR jets, is converted into leptons as opposed to
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
.
Due to the presence of a massive
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
, contributing to the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, signals tend to populate lower parts of the
|\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{lep}}|/E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
spectrum than SM backgrounds,
particularly for the compressed signals in the high
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
regions where the
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
is almost fully generated by the ISR jets.
A tight upper bound
|\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{lep}}|/E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
is therefore imposed in the low
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
bins of

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

.

After applying the selection criteria,
for the wino/bino (+) model
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}

signal sample with NLSP masses
of 200 and a mass splitting of
\Delta m=20
,
the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

,

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

, and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

regions (taking the union of the bins inside each region)
have acceptance times efficiency values of

2.2\times 10^{-5}
,
1.1\times 10^{-5}
,
3.4\times 10^{-6}
, and
6.0\times 10^{-5}
, respectively.
Similarly, for a mass splitting of
\Delta m=60
, values of

1.6\times 10^{-4}
,
1.7\times 10^{-4}
,
2.8\times 10^{-4}
, and
7.9\times 10^{-5}
are found.
The acceptance times efficiency values for the wino/bino (
-
) and higgsino model signal samples
are typically 15%–55% and 20%–60% lower, depending on the region.

8.2
Background estimation

The selection criteria for the CRs and the VRs for
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
estimation are summarised in Table
10
.
An on-shell
Z
boson (
m_{\ell\ell}\in[81,101]\penalty\
) is required to ensure orthogonality to the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

,
and an upper bound on
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
ensures orthogonality to the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

.
A lower bound on
m_{\text{T}}
is applied to suppress the
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
background.
The CRs are further split into two bins (

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

and

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}}}

), based on the absence or presence of jets,
to constrain
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
events without or with hard ISR jets separately with individual normalisation factors.

Three validation regions are defined in the region with
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}},m_{\ell\ell}<75\penalty\
, similar to

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

.
First,

VR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

and

VR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}}}

are designed to validate the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
estimation in the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

phase space.
A window in
m_{\mathrm{T}}
around the Jacobian peak (
m_{\text{T}}\in[60,90]\penalty\
) is selected to enhance
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
,
as well as to ensure the orthogonality with respect to the SRs.
Further kinematic selection criteria similar to those in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

are applied.
Two additional variables are employed in the

VR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

to suppress the signal contamination in the region.
The
W
-boson mass,
m_{\mathrm{W}}^{\text{reco\,W\hskip-0.39864ptZ}}
, is reconstructed assuming the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
topology and balanced longitudinal momenta of the
W
and
Z
bosons,
and
\Delta R(\ell_{\mathrm{W}},E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}})
is defined by
\sqrt{\scalebox{0.85}{$\eta_{\ell_{\mathrm{W}}}^{2}+\Delta\phi(\ell_{\mathrm{W}},E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}})^{2}$}}

where leptons are assigned according to the
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
approach, and
\ell_{\mathrm{W}}
is the lepton associated with the
W
boson.
Since
m_{\mathrm{W}}^{\text{reco\,W\hskip-0.39864ptZ}}
peaks around
m_{W}
with a long tail to higher masses for
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
background, while signals tend to have a flatter distribution,
m_{\mathrm{W}}^{\text{reco\,W\hskip-0.39864ptZ}}>75\penalty\
is found to effectively reduce signal contamination.

In the very low
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
region,

VR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}-{low}{m}${}_{\texttt{\tiny ll}}$}}

is used to validate the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
estimation in the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

phase space.
This region has the low-mass resonance veto applied and a lower bound on
|\textbf{p}_{\mathrm{T}}^{\mathrm{lep}}|/E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
to ensure orthogonality with the SRs.
Other kinematic cuts are loosened relative to

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

, or removed entirely, to increase the number of data events in the region.
The
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
purity is 85%–90% in the CRs and 70%–75% in the VRs.
The contamination from the benchmark signals is negligible in the CRs and below 15% in the VRs.

The

VR

t
\bar{\texttt{t}}

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

selection criteria are summarised in Table
10
.
At least one
b
-jet is required to maintain orthogonality with the SRs,

E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}>50\penalty\
is required to suppress the
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
contribution,
and the low-mass resonance background veto is applied.
The
t\bar{t}
purity in this region is approximately 65%.

The
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
background is estimated using the FF method as described in Section
6.2
.
The FF measurement region for the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection,

CR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, is summarised in Table
11
.
The
Z
-boson candidate is selected by requiring
|m_{\ell\ell}-m_{Z}|<15\penalty\
, and

E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}<40\penalty\
and
m_{\mathrm{T}}<30\penalty\
are applied to reject contamination from
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
.
Additionally
m_{3\ell}>105\penalty\
is applied to suppress
Z\rightarrow 4\ell
.
To increase the number of FNP lepton candidates at high
p_{\text{T}}
,
the overlap removal procedure described in Section
5
is modified for this FF measurement so that muons overlapping with jets are always kept.
Finally, a jet veto is applied except for events where the FNP lepton candidate is a muon with
p_{\text{T}}>30\penalty\
,
in which case
n_{\text{jets}}^{30\penalty\ }\leq 1
is required in order to account for the special muon-vs-jet overlap-removal treatment applied to this region.

The FFs are derived separately per lepton flavour of FNP lepton candidates and per signal lepton criterion, i.e. with or without applying the non-prompt BDT,
and are parameterised as a function of lepton
p_{\text{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
in the event.
Typical FF values are 0.2–0.4 (0.2–0.6) without the BDT applied,
and 0.05–0.2 (0.07–0.2) when applying the BDT,
for electrons (muons) in a
p_{\text{T}}
range of 4.5–30 (3.0–30) .
The parameterisation in
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
is used to reflect the variation of FNP lepton source with
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
, which is required in order to model the shape of fake
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
correctly.
Typically the fraction of FNP leptons originating from heavy-flavour decays varies with
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
,
in the range 20%–30% (60%–70%) for electrons (muons),
because of the neutrinos from the leptonic
b
-/
c
-decays.

The contribution of non-
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
processes is subtracted using MC simulated samples.
A small normalisation correction is applied to the
t\bar{t}
events in the simulated anti-ID region
to account for the different anti-ID lepton efficiencies in data and MC simulation.
Normalisation factors are derived separately depending on the
\smash{\ell_{\mathrm{W}}}
flavour and the
b
-jet multiplicity in the event.
They are measured using the data events in a
t\bar{t}
-enriched control region,

CR

t
\bar{\texttt{t}}

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{anti-ID}}

, and are found to be between 0.88 and 0.95.
The

CR

t
\bar{\texttt{t}}

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{anti-ID}}

selection requires there to be no SFOS lepton pair in the event, as well as
p_{\text{T}}^{\ell_{3}}>10\penalty\
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}>50\penalty\
to enhance the
t\bar{t}
purity.

Two sources of uncertainty specific to the estimation in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

are considered in addition to those described in Section
6.3
.
The FF parameterisation uncertainty is evaluated from the effect of using a different
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
binning (
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}

<50
, 50% larger bin size),
or a 3D parameterisation in lepton
p_{\text{T}}
,
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
and lepton
\eta
, additionally taking into account the dependency on lepton
\eta
.
The impact on the
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
background yields in the CRs is
\sim 5\%
, and 1%–7% in the SRs/VRs.
The uncertainty from disabling the muon-vs-jet overlap removal procedure in the FF measurement region
is assessed by comparing those FFs with alternative FFs measured with muon-vs-jet overlap removal applied for events with a FNP muon candidate of
p_{\text{T}}<30\penalty\
.
The variation in the estimated
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
yields in the SRs/CRs/VRs is found to be 5%–15%.

The yields predicted by the FF method are cross-checked in dedicated VRs enriched in FNP lepton backgrounds, as summarised in Table
11
.
The
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}

significance
selection is inverted with respect to the SRs to ensure orthogonality.
First,

VR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

and

VR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}}}

are designed to validate the yields in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

, respectively,
while

VR

FF

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}-{low}{p}${}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

aims to cross-check the modelling of FNP leptons with
p_{\text{T}}<10\penalty\
specifically.
The
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
purity is in the VRs is 50%–80%, while the contamination from signals is negligible.

Performing the background-only fit,
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
normalisation factors of

1.06\pm 0.03
(

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{0j}}}}

)
and

0.93\pm 0.03
(

CR

WZ

{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{\scalebox{0.9}{{nj}}}}

)
are determined.
Examples of kinematic distributions in the CRs and VRs, demonstrating good agreement,
are presented in Figure
7
.
Observed and expected yields for all CRs and VRs are summarised in Figure
8
.

The systematic uncertainties considered in the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection
are summarised in Figure
9
,
grouped as discussed in Section
6.3
.
As the expected yields can vary by an order of magnitude throughout the regions, bin-to-bin fluctuations are expected in both the statistical and experimental uncertainty;
these uncertainties are often dominant in bins with limited MC statistics in the phase space of the selection.
The FNP lepton uncertainty is naturally more important in bins with larger FNP lepton background contributions,
and can fluctuate in bins with few events in the corresponding anti-ID sample, such as

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

0j

b

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

b

.
The modelling uncertainty is larger in the presence of ISR jets and at higher values of
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
; the fluctuation in

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

g2

originates from the effect of the QCD scale uncertainty on the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
background.

, off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
SRs are compared with the background expectation obtained from the background-only fits described in Section
6.4
.
The results are summarised
in Tables
12
–
13
as well as visualised in Figures
10
–
11
for the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

regions,
and in Tables
14
–
15
and Figure
12
for the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

.
Post-fit distributions of key kinematic observables are shown for the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

regions in Figure
13

and for the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

regions in Figure
14
.

To illustrate the sensitivity to various
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
signals throughout the regions, representative signal MC predictions are overlaid on the figures.
The sensitivity to
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated models, when the mass difference between the
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
/
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
and
\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
is large, is driven by the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

with large
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
values.
On the other hand, when the mass splitting is close to the
Z
-boson mass,
the sensitivity is dominated by the high
H_{\text{T}}
region and moderate
m_{\mathrm{T}}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
bins of the
n_{\mathrm{jets}}=0
and low
H_{\text{T}}
regions.
For the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
-mediated scenarios the sensitivity is driven by

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{SFOS}}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{DFOS}}}

regions, with

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{DFOS}}}

-1

contributing the most.

9.1
Model-independent limits on new physics in inclusive regions

Model-independent upper limits and discovery
p
-values in the SRs are derived by performing the discovery fits as described in Section
6.4
.
The set of single-bin signal regions used in the fits, referred to as ‘inclusive SRs’,
is constructed by logically grouping adjoining, disjoint, nominal SRs of the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
,
W\hskip-1.00006pth
and off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selections.
Multiple, sometimes overlapping, regions are defined to capture signatures
with different unknown
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
shapes and jet multiplicities inclusively.
Based on the best expected discovery sensitivity and using a number of signal points covering both the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
- and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
-mediated scenarios and different mass splittings,
12 inclusive SRs are formed by merging

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

regions, creating
inc

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and
inc

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{Wh}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, respectively.
They are summarised in Table
16
.
Similarly, 17 inclusive SRs are formed by merging

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

regions, creating
inc

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

; their definitions are summarised in Table
17
.
For
inc

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, contiguous jet-veto regions are merged with jet-inclusive regions, as the
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}
shape of a signal is assumed to be insensitive to jet multiplicity.
The

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{low}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

regions are kept separate, while the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{{high}$\not{\texttt{E}}_{\texttt{\tiny T}}$}}

-

nj

regions are considered separately for
m_{\ell\ell}^{\mathrm{min}}<20\penalty\
,
as this selection provides the best sensitivity to low-mass-splitting models.

The 95% CL upper limits on the generic BSM cross section are calculated by performing a discovery fit for each target SR and its associated CRs, using pseudo-experiments.
Results are reported in Table
18
(
19
)
for the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
analysis selections (off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection).
The tables list
the observed (
N_{\text{obs}}
) and expected (
N_{\text{exp}}
) yields in the inclusive SRs,
the upper limits on the observed (
S^{95}_{\mathrm{obs}}
) and expected (
S^{95}_{\mathrm{exp}}
) number of BSM events,
and the visible cross section (
\sigma^{95}_{\mathrm{vis}}
) reflecting the product of the production cross section, the acceptance,
and the selection efficiency for a BSM process;
the
p
-value and significance (
Z
) for the background-only hypothesis are also presented.

8.1
.
The results are statistically combined with the previous results for the electroweakino regions (
SR-E
) of the two-lepton search targeting compressed mass spectra
[
18
]
, referred to as the compressed selection.
Model-dependent 95% CL exclusion limits are calculated by performing the exclusion fits as described in Section
6.4
.
When performing the combination, common experimental uncertainties are treated as correlated between regions and processes.
Theoretical uncertainties of the background and signal are treated as correlated between regions only,
while statistical uncertainties are considered uncorrelated between regions and processes.

All regions of the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, and compressed selections were explicitly designed to be orthogonal,
allowing a statistical combination of the results.
The on-shell and off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selections are orthogonal due to the
m_{\ell\ell}
and
E_{\text{T}}^{\text{miss}}
requirements,
while the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and compressed selections are orthogonal by lepton multiplicity.
Results are combined where greater exclusion power is expected over the individual results,
ignoring contributions from search regions that do not add sensitivity in a given region of phase space.
This approach results in multiple pairwise combinations of
the on-shell and off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selections,
and the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and compressed selections,
in bands of the
(\Delta m,\,m(\smash{\raisebox{-0.65pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}$ }}))
plane.

Four separate fits are performed to obtain constraints for the following simplified models:

•

the wino/bino (+)
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model
combining the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, and compressed selections,

•

the wino/bino (+)
W\hskip-1.00006pth
-mediated model
using the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
selection only,

•

the wino/bino (
-
)
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model
combining the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and compressed selections,

•

the higgsino
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model
combining the off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
and compressed selections.

For the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model in the wino/bino (+) scenario, only the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

are sensitive for mass splittings
\Delta m
above
100\penalty\
.
Conversely, the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

dominate the intermediate mass-splitting region, with sensitivity in the
\Delta m=[5,100]\penalty\
range.
In the most compressed region, the
SR-E
are important, driving the result for
\Delta m
below
10\penalty\
and adding sensitivity up to
\Delta m=50\penalty\
.
Given these contributions, the
\Delta m
range is split into five bands: [
<
8, 8–28, 28–78, 78–108,
>
108] .
In each of these bands the combination considers respectively the
SR-E
only, the
SR-E
and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

only, the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, and the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{WZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

only.
In the wino/bino (
-
) scenario, the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection is not used, and three bands are defined for the combination as [
<
8, 8–28,
>
28] .
In the higgsino scenario, the mass-splitting range is restricted to
\Delta m\leq 60\penalty\
for model consistency, and the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
selection is not used.
The combination is defined using three bands: [
<
8, 8–42,
>
42] ,
respectively considering the
SR-E
only, the
SR-E
and

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

, and the

SR
{}^{\scalebox{0.65}{{offWZ}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{}}

only.
The ranges used are illustrated for the different scenarios in Figure
15
.

10
Recursive Jigsaw Reconstruction selection and results

To follow up on an earlier ATLAS search performed
using the Recursive Jigsaw Reconstruction (RJR) technique
with the 2015–2016, 36 dataset
[
15
]
,
the search in this paper includes two signal regions in which the original search observed excesses of three-lepton events.
The original search in the two regions is repeated following the same methods, updated to use the full Run 2 dataset.
The

SR3
\ell
-Low

region targets low-mass wino/bino (+)
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
production,
while the

SR3
\ell
-ISR

region targets wino/bino (+)
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
production in association with ISR and mass differences
\Delta m
near the
Z
-boson mass.
The excesses in

SR3
\ell
-Low

and

SR3
\ell
-ISR

observed in the 36 result
correspond to local significances of 2.1
\sigma
and 3.0
\sigma
, respectively.

The RJR technique endeavours to resolve the ambiguities inherent in reconstructing original particles
for event decays including invisible particles, e.g. SUSY particles.
By analysing the event starting from the laboratory frame and boosting back to the parent particle’s rest frame,
assuming given decay chains, the technique can resolve the
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
particles.
For this search, both the standard decay tree applied to a three-lepton final state (representing the decay of pair-produced sparticles into a final state with two invisible objects and three leptons, in the laboratory frame)
and the ISR decay tree (representing the decay of an intermediate sparticle into a visible and an invisible component, recoiling from ISR activity, in the centre-of-mass frame) are considered.
Using the reconstructed leptons, jets, and missing transverse momentum as inputs,
the algorithm assigns each particle to a parent sparticle.
ISR jets are selected by minimising the invariant mass of the system formed by the candidate jets and the sparticle system, in the centre-of-mass frame.
The algorithm then determines the smallest Lorentz-invariant configuration of the particles’ four-momenta guaranteeing a non-negative mass parameter for the invisible particles.
Finally, object or frame momenta and derived variables can be considered in each of the different frames of each decay tree.

The search in the RJR selection regions follows a similar strategy
for background estimation, systematic uncertainty treatment, and statistical interpretation
to that outlined for the on-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, off-shell
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
, and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
selections in Section
6
.
For the search in

SR3
\ell
-Low

(

SR3
\ell
-ISR

), the SM diboson background is taken from MC simulation samples and normalised in a dedicated control region

CR3
\ell
-VV

(

CR3
\ell
-ISR-VV

) and validated in a validation region

VR3
\ell
-VV

(

VR3
\ell
-ISR-VV

).
The selection criteria for each of the regions follow the original search
[
15
]
,
except for an additional jet-veto (
n_{\text{jets}}=0
) in

CR3
\ell
-VV

and

VR3
\ell
-VV

which guarantees the orthogonality between the low-mass and ISR regions.
The FNP lepton background component, including
t\bar{t}
,
tW
,
WW
and
Z\text{+}\text{jets}
SM background contributions, is estimated in a data-driven way using the matrix method
[
177
]
.
The method derives the number of events with one or two FNP leptons by relating the yields for tighter (signal tagged) and looser (baseline tagged) lepton identification criteria.
The result is a function of the real-lepton identification efficiencies and the FNP lepton misidentification probabilities.
The remaining SM backgrounds, including multiboson and Higgs boson production, and top-pair production in association with a boson, are estimated from MC simulation in all analysis regions.
Beyond the treatment of experimental and theoretical systematical uncertainties following the general strategy in Section
6.3
,
uncertainties are assigned to the matrix-method FNP lepton background estimation,
accounting for limited numbers of events in the measurement region,
potentially different compositions (heavy flavour, light flavour, or conversions) between SRs and CRs,
and the uncertainty from the subtraction of prompt-lepton contributions using MC simulation samples.

Performing the background-only fit, diboson normalisation factors of 0.92
\pm
0.07 (

CR3
\ell
-VV

) and 0.92
\pm
0.05 (

CR3
\ell
-ISR-VV

) are determined.
Observed and expected yields for all CRs and VRs are summarised in Figure
18

and a summary of the considered systematic uncertainties is presented in
Figure
19
, grouped as discussed in Section
6.3
.

, adding a minus sign if the yield is below the prediction.

in the centre-of-mass (CM) frame and assuming the ISR decay tree.
Good agreement with the background-only hypothesis is observed in both SRs.
The deviations from the SM expectation as found in the 36 result are reduced and no longer significant when including the additional 103 of data from the 2017–2018 datasets.

) reflecting the product of the production cross section, the acceptance,
and the selection efficiency for a BSM process;
the
p
-value and significance (

) for the background-only hypothesis are also presented.

11
Conclusion

Results of a search for chargino–neutralino pair production decaying via
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
,
W^{*}Z^{*}
or
W\hskip-1.00006pth
into three-lepton final states are presented.
A dataset of
\sqrt{s}=13\penalty\
proton–proton collisions corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 139 , collected by the ATLAS experiment at the CERN LHC, is used.
Events with three light-flavour charged leptons and missing transverse momentum are preselected,
and three selections are developed with a signal region strategy optimised for chargino–neutralino signals decaying via
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
,
W^{*}Z^{*}
and
W\hskip-1.00006pth
, respectively.
A fourth selection targeting the chargino–neutralino signals decaying via
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
using the Recursive Jigsaw Reconstruction technique is also studied,
to follow up on the excesses observed in the previous ATLAS result using the same method and event selection.
In all the selections the data are found to be consistent with predictions of the Standard Model.
The results are interpreted for simplified models with wino or higgsino production.
A statistical combination is performed to include the result of an ATLAS search probing the final state with two soft leptons using the same dataset.

Assuming a simplified model with wino production decaying to a bino LSP, exclusion limits at 95% confidence level are placed
on the minimum
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
/
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
mass,
extending the reach of previous searches
[
17
,
14
,
15
,
21
,
16
,
18
]
.
Limits are set
at 640 for the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model signals
in the limit of massless
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
,
improving by about 140 ;
and at 300 for mass-splittings between
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
/
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
close to
m_{Z}
,
improving by about 100 .
In the case of a mass splitting of 5–90 ,
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
/
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
masses up to 200–300 for the
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model are excluded.
The limit extends down to a smallest mass splitting of 2 for a
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
mass of 100 .
The dependency on a model parameter – the sign of the
\smash{m_{\text{eig}}}(\smash{\raisebox{-0.65pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}$}}$ }}\hskip-1.79993pt)\penalty\hskip 0.50003pt\times\penalty\hskip 0.50003pt\smash{m_{\text{eig}}}(\smash{\raisebox{-0.65pt}{$\mathchoice{\displaystyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\textstyle\raise 1.72218pt\hbox{$\textstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptstyle\raise 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}{\scriptscriptstyle\raise 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\tilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}$}}$ }}\hskip-1.79993pt)
product – is also tested,
and comparable limits are found for the two scenarios.
For the
W\hskip-1.00006pth
-mediated model signals, the limit
on the minimum
\widetilde{\chi}^{\pm}_{1}
/
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
mass is set at 190 , for
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{1}
masses below 20 .

Limits are also set for simplified models with a higgsino LSP triplet,
for the first time including results from three-lepton final states,
which increases sensitivity to scenarios with moderate mass splittings.
Combined with the two-lepton analysis targeting compressed mass spectra,
the exclusion limits at 95% confidence level are placed on the minimum
\widetilde{\chi}^{0}_{2}
mass
up to 210 for
W\hskip-1.00006ptZ
-mediated model signals with a mass splitting of 2–60 .
In these models, searches in the three-lepton final state enhance the sensitivity in the experimentally challenging region with mass splitting greater than 30 .
